<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 237. TELECOMMUNICATIONS</h2>
      
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.01">
        <h1 class="shn">237.01 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this chapter, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 7]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Independent telephone company.</span></h2>
          <p>"Independent telephone company" means a telephone company organized and operating under chapter 301 or 302A or authorized to do business in Minnesota under chapter 303 as of January 1, 1983, and providing local exchange service to fewer than 30,000 subscribers within the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Radio common carrier.</span></h2>
          <p>"Radio common carrier" means a person, firm, association, or corporation which owns, operates, or otherwise furnishes to the public any paging or other mobile telecommunications service by means of the use of radio signals and connection to a telephone network.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/557/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 557 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications carrier.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications carrier" means a person, firm, association, or corporation authorized to furnish one or more of the following telephone services to the public, but not otherwise authorized to furnish local exchange service: (1) interexchange telephone service; (2) local telephone service pursuant to a certificate granted under the authority of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16#stat.237.16.4">237.16, subdivision 4</a>, before August 1, 1995; or (3) local service pursuant to a certificate granted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a>, for the first time after August 1, 1995, except if granted to a successor to a telephone company otherwise authorized to furnish local exchange service. Telecommunications carrier does not include entities that derive more than 50 percent of their revenues from operator services provided to transient locations such as hotels, motels, and hospitals. In addition, telecommunications carrier does not include entities that provide centralized equal access services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications service" means the offering of telecommunications for a fee directly to the public or to such classes of users as to be effectively available to the public regardless of the facilities used.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.6b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6b.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications service provider.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications service provider" means any provider of telecommunications service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Telephone company.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telephone company" means and applies to any person, firm, association or any corporation, private or municipal, owning or operating any telephone line or telephone exchange for hire, wholly or partly within this state, or furnishing any telephone service to the public.</p>
          <p>A "telephone company" does not include a radio common carrier as defined in subdivision 4. A telephone company which also conforms with the definition of a radio common carrier is subject to regulation as a telephone company. However, none of chapter 237 applies to telephone company activities which conform to the definition of a radio common carrier.</p>
          <p>A "telephone company" does not include a telecommunications carrier as defined in subdivision 6, except that a telecommunications carrier is a telephone company for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/222.36">222.36</a>. A telephone company is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.74">237.74</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Local exchange carrier.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local exchange carrier" means a telephone company or telecommunications carrier providing local exchange service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.01.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Wholesale transport provider.</span></h2>
          <p>"Wholesale transport provider" means any person, firm, association, or corporation that carries, delivers, routes, or transports any telecommunications service subject to the commission's jurisdiction, directly or indirectly, but is not certified in Minnesota to provide retail telecommunications service to the public.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">5287</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1915/0/152/#laws.0.2.0">1915 c 152 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/248/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 248 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/451/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 451 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/557/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 557 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/22/#laws.0.1.0">1985 c 22 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.18.0">1987 c 340 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/268/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 268 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 156 s 1</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/261/#laws.1.1.0">2004 c 261 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/115/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 115 s 1</a>-3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.011">
        <h1 class="shn">237.011 TELECOMMUNICATIONS GOALS.</h1>
        <p>The following are state goals that should be considered as the commission executes its regulatory duties with respect to telecommunication services:</p>
        <p>(1) supporting universal service;</p>
        <p>(2) maintaining just and reasonable rates;</p>
        <p>(3) encouraging economically efficient deployment of infrastructure for higher speed telecommunication services and greater capacity for voice, video, and data transmission;</p>
        <p>(4) encouraging fair and reasonable competition for local exchange telephone service in a competitively neutral regulatory manner;</p>
        <p>(5) maintaining or improving quality of service;</p>
        <p>(6) promoting customer choice;</p>
        <p>(7) ensuring consumer protections are maintained in the transition to a competitive market for local telecommunications service; and</p>
        <p>(8) encouraging voluntary resolution of issues between and among competing providers and discouraging litigation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/223/#laws.0.2.0">1997 c 223 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.012">
        <h1 class="shn">237.012 BROADBAND GOALS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.012.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Universal access and high-speed goal.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a state goal that:</p>
          <p>(1) no later than 2022, all Minnesota businesses and homes have access to high-speed broadband that provides minimum download speeds of at least 25 megabits per second and minimum upload speeds of at least three megabits per second; and</p>
          <p>(2) no later than 2026, all Minnesota businesses and homes have access to at least one provider of broadband with download speeds of at least 100 megabits per second and upload speeds of at least 20 megabits per second.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.012.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State broadband leadership position.</span></h2>
          <p>It is a goal of the state that by 2022 and thereafter, the state be in:</p>
          <p>(1) the top five states of the United States for broadband speed universally accessible to residents and businesses;</p>
          <p>(2) the top five states for broadband access; and</p>
          <p>(3) the top 15 when compared to countries globally for broadband penetration.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.012.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/85/#laws.3.28.0">2013 c 85 art 3 s 28</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.012.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/277/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 277 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.5.9.0">2016 c 189 art 5 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.02">
        <h1 class="shn">237.02 GENERAL AUTHORITY OF DEPARTMENT AND COMMISSION; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <p>The Department of Commerce and the Public Utilities Commission are hereby vested with the same jurisdiction and supervisory power over telephone and telecommunications companies doing business in this state as the commission's predecessor, the railroad and warehouse commission, had over railroad and express companies. The definitions set forth in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216A.02">216A.02</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.02">216B.02</a> also apply to this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.02.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">5286</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1915/0/152/#laws.0.1.0">1915 c 152 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/25/#laws.0.67.0">1971 c 25 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.118.0">1980 c 614 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.53.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 53</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.025">
        <h1 class="shn">237.025 COMPETITIVE MARKET REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, a "competitive service provider" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a wireless voice service provider; or</p>
          <p>(2) any other provider of local voice service who owns a substantial proportion of the last-mile or loop facilities delivering service to a majority of households in an exchange service area, without regard to the technology used to deliver the service.</p>
          <p class="bl">"Competitive service provider" does not include:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) a provider using satellite technology;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) a wireless voice service provider who resells voice services purchased at wholesale;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) a competitive local exchange carrier, as defined in Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7811.0100">7811.0100</a>, subpart 12, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7812.0100">7812.0100</a>, subpart 12, who does not own a substantial proportion of the last-mile or loop facilities over which they provide local voice service;</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) an over-the-top VOIP provider; or</p>
          <p class="in">(v) a local exchange carrier petitioning to be regulated under this section or any affiliate of the petitioning local exchange carrier.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Exchange service area" has the meaning given in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7810.0100">7810.0100</a>, subpart 15.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) "Local exchange carrier" means a telephone company, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01#stat.237.01.7">237.01, subdivision 7</a>, or the corporate successor to a telephone company, providing local telephone service pursuant to a certificate granted by the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a> before August 1, 1995.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) "Over-the-top VOIP provider" means a VOIP provider that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) requires the end user to obtain broadband transmission from a third-party provider; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) has no business relationship with the provider of the Internet connection used by the VOIP provider to deliver voice service.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) "VOIP" or "Voice over Internet Protocol" means any service that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) enables real-time two-way voice communications that originate from or terminate at the user's location in Internet protocol or any successor protocol; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) permits users to receive calls that originate on the public switched telephone network and terminate calls to the public switched telephone network.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Petition.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A local exchange carrier may petition the commission to have its residential voice services and business voice services to customers subscribing to three or fewer business lines regulated under this section in any exchange service area in which the carrier provides local voice service. The petition must be served on the commission, the department, the Office of the Attorney General, all telephone companies and telecommunications carriers with which the local exchange carrier has an interconnection agreement, and any other person designated by the commission.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A petition filed under this subdivision must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a list of exchange service areas in which the local exchange carrier is seeking to be regulated under this section;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the local services offered by the local exchange carrier in each exchange service area;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) a list of competitive service providers in each exchange service area;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) a description of affiliate relationships the petitioning local exchange carrier has with any provider of local service in each exchange service area;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) documentation demonstrating the local exchange carrier's loss of local voice service customers to unaffiliated competitive service providers in each exchange service area over, at a minimum, the previous five years;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) evidence demonstrating that the local exchange carrier satisfies the competitive criteria under subdivision 4 in each exchange service area; and</p>
          <p class="in">(7) other information requested by the commission that is relevant to the applicable competitive criteria under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Process; objection; review.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A petition by a local exchange carrier seeking to be regulated under this section shall be reviewed by the commission as provided under this subdivision.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A party objecting to a local exchange carrier's petition filed under this section must file an objection within 45 days.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) If no party objects to a petitioning local exchange carrier's proposed election within 45 days of the filing of the petition, the petition is deemed approved.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) If a party raises an objection to a local exchange carrier's petition, the commission must provide interested parties and the petitioning carrier an opportunity to comment on the merits of the petition.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) The commission shall make a final determination regarding a petition filed under this section within 180 days of the date all information required under subdivision 2 was submitted.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) In reviewing the petition, the commission may request additional information from the petitioning local exchange carrier and other service providers under the commission's jurisdiction that provide service in the relevant exchange service area.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Competitive criteria.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission shall approve a petition under this section if a petitioning local exchange carrier demonstrates to the commission's satisfaction that:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) it serves fewer than 50 percent of the households in an exchange service area, and at least 60 percent of households in the exchange service area can choose voice service from at least one additional unaffiliated competitive service provider; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) it serves more than 50 percent of the households in an exchange service area, and:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) at least 60 percent of households in the exchange service area can choose voice service from at least one additional unaffiliated competitive service provider;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) no significant economic, technological, or other barriers to market entry and exit exist;</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) no single provider has the ability to maintain prices above competitive levels for a significant period of time or otherwise deter competition; and</p>
          <p class="in">(iv) the petitioning local exchange carrier will continue to offer basic local service, as defined in subdivision 8, consistent with its tariffs in effect at the time of its petition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The burden of proof to show that the competitive criteria of subdivision 4 have been met shall be on the petitioning local exchange carrier.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Market regulation and consumer protection.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A local exchange carrier that has received approval from the commission to be regulated under this section in one or more of its exchange service areas shall be subject to regulation in those approved exchange service areas as a telecommunications carrier under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.035">237.035</a>, and as a competitive local exchange carrier under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7811.2210">7811.2210</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7812.2210">7812.2210</a>, as applicable. Nothing in this section shall be construed to provide or imply that a local exchange carrier regulated under this section is exempted from Minnesota Statutes and Minnesota Rules applying to competitive local exchange carriers, including, but not limited to:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.66">237.66</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.661">237.661</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.663">237.663</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.665">237.665</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.69">237.69</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.71">237.71</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) Minnesota Rules, chapter 7810.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Regulation under this section is effective 30 days after a petition is deemed approved under subdivision 3 or approved by the commission under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Effect on existing alternative form of regulation plans.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Approval of a petition for regulation under this section does not relieve an incumbent local exchange carrier of its obligation to fulfill all the terms of an existing alternative form of regulation plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.774">237.774</a> for the duration of the plan.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Following the expiration of the alternative form of regulation plan, an incumbent local exchange carrier whose petition is approved by the commission shall be subject to applicable provisions of this section in the approved local exchange areas.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Basic local service protection.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "basic local service" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) single party voice-grade service and touch-tone capability;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) access to the public switched network;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) 911 or enhanced 911 access; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) telecommunications relay service capability and access necessary to comply with state and federal regulations.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Except as provided in paragraph (d) below:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) prior to January 1, 2018, basic local service rates may not be increased beyond the rates in effect on May 20, 2016;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) on or after January 1, 2018, basic local service rates for residential customers and business customers subscribing to three or fewer lines may not be increased on a per month basis by more than $2.00. Any new basic local service rate established must remain in effect for a minimum of 12 months. Until December 31, 2022, basic local service rates may not exceed $25.00; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) on or after January 1, 2023, basic local service rates for residential customers and business customers subscribing to three or fewer lines may not be increased on a per month basis by more than $2.00. Any new basic local service rate established must remain in effect for a minimum of 12 months. Any rate change effective after January 1, 2023, is deemed approved 90 days after the commission's receiving notice of the rate change, unless the commission begins an investigation pursuant to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7811.2210">7811.2210</a>, subpart 17, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7812.2210">7812.2210</a>, subpart 17, to determine if the rate will result in substantial customer harm, in which case the commission must make a final determination within 180 days of receiving notice of the rate change.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Notwithstanding any statute or rule to the contrary, basic local service rates must be uniform within an exchange service area.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The provisions of paragraph (b) shall not apply to a rate of return carrier, as defined in Code of Federal Regulations, title 47, section 54.5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Obligation to serve.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Nothing in this section affects the obligation of a local exchange carrier that petitions the commission to be regulated under this section to provide service to customers, when requested, in accordance with this chapter, commission rules, and its duly authorized tariffs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Relation to other law.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section restricts, creates, expands, or otherwise affects or modifies:</p>
          <p>(1) any entity's obligations or rights or the commission's authority under the Federal Communications Act of 1934, United States Code, title 47, sections 251 and 252;</p>
          <p>(2) any commission authority or existing orders related to wholesale services;</p>
          <p>(3) any commission jurisdiction:</p>
          <p>(i) over intrastate switched access rates, terms, and conditions, including the implementation of federal law with respect to intercarrier compensation, and</p>
          <p>(ii) to address or affect the resolution of disputes regarding intercarrier compensation; or</p>
          <p>(4) the rights of any entity, or the authority of the commission and local government authorities, with respect to the use and regulation of public rights-of-way under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.162">237.162</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.163">237.163</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.025.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Reexamining competitiveness of markets.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commission may, upon petition or on its own motion, open a proceeding to examine whether the competitive criteria in subdivision 4 continue to be met in an exchange service area in which a local exchange carrier previously received commission approval to be regulated under this section. If the commission determines that the competitive criteria are no longer met, it shall determine the appropriate level of regulation for that provider in that exchange service area.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.025.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/115/#laws.0.4.0">2016 c 115 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.03">
        <h1 class="shn">237.03 SCOPE OF LAW.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, all the provisions of Revised Laws 1905, chapter 28, and acts amendatory thereof applying to railroad and express companies, shall insofar as the same are applicable apply also to telephone companies.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.03.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">5288</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1915/0/152/#laws.0.3.0">1915 c 152 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.035">
        <h1 class="shn">237.035 TELECOMMUNICATIONS CARRIER EXEMPTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Telecommunications carriers are subject to regulation under this chapter only to the extent required under paragraphs (b) to (e).</p>
        <p>(b) Telecommunications carriers shall comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.121">237.121</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.74">237.74</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) Telecommunications carriers shall comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a>, subdivisions 8 and 9.</p>
        <p>(d) To the extent a telecommunications carrier offers local service, it shall obtain a certificate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a> for that local service.</p>
        <p>(e) In addition, a telecommunications carrier's local service is subject to this chapter except that:</p>
        <p>(1) a telecommunications carrier is not subject to rate-of-return or earnings investigations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.075">237.075</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>; and</p>
        <p>(2) a telecommunications carrier is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.22">237.22</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.035.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/268/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 268 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 156 s 2</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.036">
        <h1 class="shn">237.036 COIN-OPERATED OR PUBLIC PAY TELEPHONES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Neither commission approval nor a commission certificate is required to:</p>
        <p>(1) site a coin-operated or public pay telephone in the state; or</p>
        <p>(2) implement changes in service, services offered, rates, or location regarding a coin-operated or public pay telephone. Registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.64">237.64</a> is required to own or operate a coin-operated or public pay telephone in the state.</p>
        <p>(b) This section does not change the authority of other state or local government entities to regulate aspects of coin-operated or public pay telephone ownership, location, or operation; however, an entity may not regulate aspects of these services that it did not regulate prior to May 26, 1999. The commission shall retain the authority delegated to it under federal and state law to protect the public interest with regard to coin-operated or public pay telephones.</p>
        <p>(c) Owners and operators of coin-operated or public pay telephones are exempt from sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.06">237.06</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.07">237.07</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.075">237.075</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.09">237.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.23">237.23</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.295">237.295</a>, and the annual reporting requirement of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.11">237.11</a>.</p>
        <p>(d) Owners of coin-operated or public pay telephones shall:</p>
        <p>(1) provide immediate coin-free access, to the extent technically feasible, to 911 emergency service or to another approved emergency service; and</p>
        <p>(2) provide free access to the telecommunications relay service for people with communication disabilities.</p>
        <p>(e) Owners of coin-operated or public pay telephones must post at each coin-operated or public pay telephone location:</p>
        <p>(1) customer service and complaint information, including the name, address, and telephone number of the owner of the coin-operated or public pay telephone and the operator service handling calls from the coin-operated or public pay telephone; a toll-free number of the appropriate telephone company for the resolution of complaints; and the toll-free number of the public utilities commission; and</p>
        <p>(2) a toll-free number at which consumers can obtain pricing information regarding rates, charges, terms, and conditions of local and long-distance calls.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.036.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/224/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 224 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/62/#laws.0.14.0">2013 c 62 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.1.30.0">2014 c 222 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">RATES AND PRACTICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.04">
        <h1 class="shn">237.04 WIRE CROSSING OR PARALLELING UTILITY LINE; RULES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The department, upon the complaint of any person, railroad, municipal utility, cooperative electric association, telephone company, telecommunications carrier, cable company, fiber optic carrier, or other public utility claiming to be injuriously affected or subjected to hazard by any crossing or paralleling of the lines of any railroad or other similar public service corporation, constructed or about to be constructed, shall, after a hearing, make such order and prescribe such terms and conditions for the construction, maintenance, and operation of the lines in question as may be just and reasonable.</p>
        <p>(b) The department may, upon request of any municipal utility, electric cooperative association, public utility, telephone company, telecommunications carrier, cable company, or fiber optic carrier determine the just and reasonable charge which a railroad, or owner of an abandoned railroad right-of-way, other than the state or a regional railroad authority, can prescribe for a new or existing crossing of a railroad right-of-way by any telephone, telegraph, telecommunications, cable, fiber optic, electric, or gas line, or new or existing telephone, telegraph, telecommunications, cable, fiber optic, electric, or gas line more or less paralleling a railroad right-of-way, based on the diminution in value caused by the crossing or paralleling of the right-of-way by the telephone, telegraph, telecommunications, cable, fiber optic, electric, or gas line. This section shall not be construed to eliminate the right of a public utility, municipal utility, or electric cooperative association to have any of the foregoing issues determined pursuant to an eminent domain proceeding commenced under chapter 117. Unless the railroad, or owner of an abandoned railroad right-of-way, other than the state or a regional railroad authority, asserts in writing that the proposed crossing or paralleling is a serious threat to the safe operations of the railroad or to the current use of the railroad right-of-way, a crossing can be constructed following filing of the requested action with the department, pending review of the requested action by the department.</p>
        <p>(c) The department shall assess the cost of reviewing the requested action, and of determining a just and reasonable charge, equally among the parties.</p>
        <p>(d) For the purposes of this section, "parallel" or "paralleling" means that the relevant utility facilities run adjacent to and alongside the lines of a railroad for no more than one mile, or another distance agreed to by the parties, before the utility facilities cross the railroad lines, terminate, or exit the railroad right-of-way.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">4718-1</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1925/0/152/#laws.0.1.0">1925 c 152 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/25/#laws.0.67.0">1971 c 25 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/123/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 123 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/8/#laws.2.61.0">1Sp2001 c 8 art 2 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.1.31.0">2014 c 222 art 1 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.045">
        <h1 class="shn">237.045 RAILROAD RIGHTS-OF-WAY; CROSSING OR PARALLELING BY UTILITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Crossing" means a utility facility constructed over, under, or across a railroad right-of-way. The term does not include longitudinal occupancy of railroad right-of-way.</p>
          <p>(c) "Facility" or "utility facility" means any item of personal property placed over, across, or underground for use in connection with the storage or conveyance of:</p>
          <p>(1) water;</p>
          <p>(2) sewage;</p>
          <p>(3) electronic, telephone, or telegraphic communications;</p>
          <p>(4) fiber optics;</p>
          <p>(5) cable television;</p>
          <p>(6) electric energy;</p>
          <p>(7) oil;</p>
          <p>(8) natural gas; or</p>
          <p>(9) hazardous liquids.</p>
          <p class="bl">Facility includes, but is not limited to, pipes, sewers, conduits, cables, valves, lines, wires, manholes, and attachments.</p>
          <p>(d) "Parallel" or "paralleling" means a utility facility that runs adjacent to and alongside the lines of a railroad for no more than one mile, or another distance agreed to by the parties, after which the utility facility crosses the railroad lines, terminates, or exits the railroad right-of-way.</p>
          <p>(e) "Railroad" means any association, corporation, or other entity engaged in operating a common carrier by rail, or its agents or assigns, including any entity responsible for the management of crossings or collection of crossing fees.</p>
          <p>(f) "Utility" means cooperative electric association, electric utility, public utility, transmission company, gas utility, municipal utility, municipal power agency, municipality, joint action agency, pipeline company, rural water system, or telephone, telegraph, telecommunications, cable, or fiber optic carrier. Utility includes contractors or agents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section applies to:</p>
          <p>(1) any crossing in existence before July 1, 2016, if an agreement concerning the crossing has expired or has been terminated. In such instance, if the collective amount that equals or exceeds the standard crossing fee under subdivision 6 has been paid to the railroad during the existence of the crossing, no additional fee is required; and</p>
          <p>(2) any crossing commenced on or after July 1, 2016.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to a crossing or paralleling of a large energy facility, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.2421#stat.216B.2421.2">216B.2421, subdivision 2</a>, regardless of length.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Right-of-way crossing; application for permission.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any utility that intends to place a facility across or upon a railroad right-of-way shall request prior permission from the railroad.</p>
          <p>(b) The request must be in the form of a completed crossing application, including an engineering design showing the location of the proposed crossing and the railroad's property, tracks, and wires that the utility will cross. The engineering design must conform with guidelines published in the most recent edition of the (1) National Electric Safety Code, or (2) Manual for Railway Engineering of the American Railway Engineering and Maintenance-of-Way Association. The utility must submit the crossing application on a form provided or approved by the railroad, if available.</p>
          <p>(c) The application must be accompanied by the standard crossing fee specified in subdivision 6 and evidence of insurance as required in subdivision 7. The utility must send the application to the railroad by certified mail, with return receipt requested.</p>
          <p>(d) Within 15 calendar days of receipt of an application that is not complete, the railroad must inform the applicant regarding any additional necessary information and submittals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Inductive interference study.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A railroad may require an electric utility to conduct an inductive interference study if:</p>
          <p>(1) the facility is for an electric energy transmission line of at least 125 kilovolts; and</p>
          <p>(2) in accordance with guidelines in the National Electric Safety Code and the Manual for Railway Engineering of the American Railway Engineering and Maintenance-of-Way Association, the railroad reasonably determines that the proposed facility poses a material possibility of creating induction issues or interference with railroad property.</p>
          <p>(b) The utility must arrange and pay for the study, perform and pay for any costs of modifications to the proposed facility, and pay for any costs of modifications to railroad property that are necessary to ensure safe and reliable railroad operations. The study must be performed by a qualified engineer approved by the railroad.</p>
          <p>(c) A utility facility for which an inductive interference study has been performed under this subdivision may not be energized until at least 30 calendar days after the railroad receives notice from the utility that the facility is ready to be energized. Within 30 days of receiving notice that the facility is ready to be energized, the railroad shall conduct any appropriate tests to ensure that there will not be any interference with safe operation of the railroad following energization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Right-of-way crossing; construction.</span></h2>
          <p>Beginning 35 calendar days after the receipt by the railroad of a completed crossing application, crossing fee, and certificate of insurance, the utility may commence the construction of the crossing unless the railroad notifies the utility in writing that the proposed crossing or paralleling is a serious threat to the safe operations of the railroad or to the current use of the railroad right-of-way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Standard crossing fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless otherwise agreed by the parties or determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.04">237.04</a>, a utility that crosses a railroad right-of-way, other than a crossing within a public right-of-way, must pay the railroad a onetime standard crossing fee of $1,250, adjusted as provided in paragraph (e), for each crossing. Except as otherwise provided in this subdivision, the standard crossing fee is paid in lieu of any license, permit, application, processing fee, or any other fee or charge to reimburse the railroad for direct expenses incurred by the railroad as a result of the crossing. No other fee or charge may be assessed to the utility by the railroad.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the standard crossing fee, the utility shall also reimburse the railroad for any reasonable and necessary flagging expense associated with a crossing, based on the railroad traffic at the crossing.</p>
          <p>(c) No crossing fee is required if the crossing is located within a public right-of-way.</p>
          <p>(d) The placement of a single conduit and its content is a single facility. No additional fees are payable based on the individual fibers, wires, lines, or other items contained within the conduit.</p>
          <p>(e) Annually each May 1, the standard crossing fee under paragraph (a) must be adjusted based on the percentage change in the annual average producer price index for the preceding year compared to the year prior to the preceding year. Each adjustment is effective for applications submitted on or after June 1. The producer price index is final demand, finished consumer energy goods, as prepared by the Bureau of Labor Statistics of the United States Department of Labor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Certificate of insurance; coverage.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The certificate of insurance or coverage submitted by:</p>
          <p>(1) a municipal utility or municipality must include commercial general liability insurance or an equivalent form with a limit of at least $1,000,000 for each occurrence and an aggregate of at least $2,000,000;</p>
          <p>(2) a utility providing natural gas service must include commercial general liability insurance with a combined single limit of at least $5,000,000 for each occurrence and an aggregate limit of at least $10,000,000; or</p>
          <p>(3) a utility not specified in clauses (1) and (2) must include commercial general liability insurance with a combined single limit of at least $2,000,000 for each occurrence and an aggregate limit of at least $6,000,000.</p>
          <p>(b) The railroad may require protective liability insurance with a combined single limit of $2,000,000 for each occurrence and $6,000,000 aggregate. The coverage may be provided by a blanket railroad protective liability insurance policy if the coverage, including the coverage limits, applies separately to each individual crossing. The coverage is required only during the period of construction, repair, or replacement of the facility.</p>
          <p>(c) The insurance coverage under paragraphs (a) and (b) must not contain an exclusion or limitation related to railroads or to activities within 50 feet of railroad property.</p>
          <p>(d) The certificate of insurance must be from an insurer of the utility's choosing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Objection to crossing; petition to Public Utilities Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a railroad objects to the proposed crossing or paralleling due to the proposal being a serious threat to the safe operations of the railroad or to the current use of the railroad right-of-way, the railroad must notify the utility of the objection and the specific basis for the objection. The railroad shall send the notice of objection to the utility by certified mail, with return receipt requested.</p>
          <p>(b) If the parties are unable to resolve the objection, either party may petition the Public Utilities Commission for assistance via mediation or arbitration of the disputed crossing application. The petition must be filed within 60 days of receipt of the objection. Before filing a petition, the parties shall make good faith efforts to resolve the objection.</p>
          <p>(c) If a petition is filed, the Public Utilities Commission must issue an order within 120 days of filing of the petition. The order may be appealed under chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.27">216B.27</a>. The Public Utilities Commission must assess the costs associated with a petition equitably among the parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Additional requirements; objection and petition to Public Utilities Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a railroad imposes additional requirements on a utility for crossing its lines, other than the proposed crossing being a serious threat to the safe operations of the railroad or to the current use of the railroad right-of-way, the utility may object to one or more of the requirements. If it objects, the utility shall provide notice of the objection and the specific basis for the objection to the railroad by certified mail, with return receipt requested.</p>
          <p>(b) If the parties are unable to resolve the objection, either party may petition the Public Utilities Commission for resolution or modification of the additional requirements. The petition must be filed within 60 days of receipt of the objection. Before filing a petition, the parties shall make good faith efforts to resolve the objection.</p>
          <p>(c) If a petition is filed, the Public Utilities Commission shall determine, after notice and opportunity for hearing, whether special circumstances exist that necessitate additional requirements for the placement of the crossing. The Public Utilities Commission must issue an order within 120 days of filing of the petition. The order may be appealed under chapter 14 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.27">216B.27</a>. The Public Utilities Commission shall assess the costs associated with a petition equitably among the parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Operational relocation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A railroad may require a utility to relocate a facility when the railroad determines that relocation is essential to accommodate railroad operations, and the relocation is not arbitrary or unreasonable. Before agreeing to the relocation, a utility may require a railroad to provide a statement and supporting documentation identifying the operational necessity for requesting the relocation. A utility must perform the relocation within a reasonable period of time following the agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) Relocation is at the expense of the small utility. A standard fee under subdivision 6 may not be imposed for relocation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.045.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Existing agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section prevents a railroad and a utility from continuing under an existing agreement, or from otherwise negotiating the terms and conditions applicable to a crossing or the resolution of any disputes relating to the crossing. A utility may elect to undertake a crossing or paralleling under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.04">237.04</a>. Nothing in this section impairs the authority of a utility to secure crossing rights by easement through exercise of the power of eminent domain.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.045.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/180/#laws.0.2.0">2016 c 180 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.05">
        <h1 class="shn">237.05 ENFORCEMENT AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <p>The department shall see that the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.04">237.04</a> are enforced; and, for that purpose shall have power to cause the removal or reconstruction of such telephone, telegraph, electric light, power, or other electric wires of any kind crossing or paralleling such other lines and not in accordance with the orders and rules issued by the department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">4718-2</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1925/0/152/#laws.0.2.0">1925 c 152 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/25/#laws.0.67.0">1971 c 25 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.06">
        <h1 class="shn">237.06 RATES AND DEPOSITS.</h1>
        <p>It shall be the duty of every telephone company to furnish reasonably adequate service and facilities for the accommodation of the public, and its rates, tolls, and charges shall be fair and reasonable for the intrastate use thereof. All unreasonable rates, tolls, and charges are hereby declared to be unlawful. Any telephone company may include in its charges a reasonable deposit fee for facilities furnished.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">5289</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1915/0/152/#laws.0.4.0">1915 c 152 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1957/0/523/#laws.0.0.0">1957 c 523</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/261/#laws.2.1.0">2004 c 261 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.065">
        <h1 class="shn">237.065 RATE FOR SCHOOL OR PURCHASING COOPERATIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.065.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Basic service; flat rate.</span></h2>
          <p>Each telephone company that provides local telephone service in a service area that includes a school that has classes within the range from kindergarten to 12th grade shall provide, upon request, additional service to the school that is sufficient to ensure access to basic telephone service from each classroom and other areas within the school, as determined by the school board. Each company shall set a flat rate for this additional service that is less than the company's flat rate for an access line for a business and the same as or greater than the company's flat rate for an access line for a residence in the same local telephone service exchange. When a company's flat rates for businesses and residences are the same, the company shall use the residential rate for service to schools under this section. The rate required under this section is available only for a school that installs additional service that includes access to basic telephone service from each classroom and other areas within the school, as determined by the school board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.065.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Basic and advanced telecommunication service; reduced rate.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.09">237.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.14">237.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.60#stat.237.60.3">237.60, subdivision 3</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.74">237.74</a>, each telephone company and telecommunications carrier that provides local telephone service in a service area that includes a school that has classes within the range from kindergarten to grade 12, a public library, or a telecommunication services purchasing cooperative may provide, upon request, basic and advanced telecommunication services at reduced or no cost to that school, library, or may provide, upon request, advanced telecommunication services at reduced wholesale rates to the members of a telecommunication services purchasing cooperative. For purposes of this section, a "telecommunication services purchasing cooperative" means a cooperative organized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/308A.210">308A.210</a>. A school or library receiving telecommunications services at reduced or no cost may not resell or sublease the discounted services. No members of a telecommunication services purchasing cooperative may resell or sublease the discounted services. A purchasing cooperative is not required to negotiate or provide a uniform rate for its members. Telecommunications services shall be provided in accordance with Public Law 104-104, and the regulations of the Federal Communications Commission adopted under the act.</p>
          <p>(b) An agent that provides telecommunications services to a school or library may request the favorable rate on behalf of and for the exclusive benefit of the school or library. The school or library must authorize the agent to make the request of the local telephone company or telecommunications carrier. The telephone company or telecommunications carrier is not required to offer the same price discount to the agent that it would offer to the school district or library. An agent that receives a price discount for telecommunications services on behalf of a school or library may only resell or sublease the discounted services to that school or library.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purposes of this subdivision, "school" includes a public school as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.05">120A.05</a>, nonpublic, and church or religious organization schools that provide instruction in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.41">120A.41</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.065.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.35.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.12.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 12 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.12.5.0">1996 c 412 art 12 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/208/#laws.0.1.0">1997 c 208 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.47.0">2001 c 7 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.066">
        <h1 class="shn">237.066 STATE GOVERNMENT PRICING PLANS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.066.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>A state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan is authorized and found to be in the public interest as it will:</p>
          <p>(1) provide and ensure availability of high-quality, technologically advanced telecommunications services at a reasonable cost to the state or Tribal government; and</p>
          <p>(2) further the state telecommunications goals as set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.011">237.011</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.066.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program participation.</span></h2>
          <p>A state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan may be available to serve individually or collectively: state agencies; Tribal governments; educational institutions, including public schools and Tribal schools complying with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.05#stat.120A.05.9">120A.05, subdivision 9</a>, 11, 13, or 17, and nonpublic schools complying with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.24">120A.24</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.41">120A.41</a>; private colleges; public corporations; and political subdivisions of the state or a Tribal Nation. Plans shall be available to carry out the commissioner of administration's duties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.17">16E.17</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.18">16E.18 </a>and shall also be available to those entities not using the commissioner for contracting for telecommunications services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.066.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rates.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.09">237.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.14">237.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.60#stat.237.60.3">237.60, subdivision 3</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.74">237.74</a>, a telephone company or a telecommunications carrier may, individually or in cooperation with other telephone companies or telecommunications carriers, develop and offer basic or advanced telecommunications services at discounted or reduced rates as a state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan. Any telecommunications services provided under any state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan shall be used exclusively by the entities described in subdivision 2 subject to the plan solely for the entities' own use and shall not be made available to any other entities by resale, sublease, or in any other way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.066.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Applicability to other customers.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company or telecommunications carrier providing telecommunications services under a state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan is not required to provide any other person or entity those services at the rates made available to the state or Tribal government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.066.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commission review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The terms and conditions of any state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan must be submitted to the commission for review and approval within 90 days before implementation to:</p>
          <p>(1) ensure that the terms and conditions benefit the state or Tribal Nation and not any private entity;</p>
          <p>(2) ensure that the rates for any telecommunications service in any state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan are at or below any applicable tariffed rates; and</p>
          <p>(3) ensure that the state telecommunications or Tribal government pricing plan meets the requirements of this section and is in the public interest.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission shall reject any state government or Tribal government telecommunications pricing plan that does not meet the criteria in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.066.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/228/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 228 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.5.23.0">2005 c 156 art 5 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/57/#laws.5.2.0">2023 c 57 art 5 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.067">
        <h1 class="shn">237.067 ESTABLISHMENT EXEMPT FROM REGULATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.067.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "establishment" means an individual hotel, motel, restaurant, lodging house, boarding house, resort, or place of refreshment licensed under chapter 157.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.067.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemption; conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>An establishment that provides telephone service to patrons on the premises of the establishment is not subject to regulation under this chapter, except that the establishment:</p>
          <p>(1) shall comply with the requirement of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.06">237.06</a> that rates charged must be fair and reasonable;</p>
          <p>(2) shall provide notice of charges and service providers to patrons as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325F.99">325F.99</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) is subject to the complaint and investigation procedures of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.067.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/154/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 154 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.068"><b>237.068</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.1.58.0">2014 c 222 art 1 s 58</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.069">
        <h1 class="shn">237.069 TRACER; HARASSING TELEPHONE CALL; RULES.</h1>
        <p>The commission shall adopt rules to govern how telephone companies respond to requests for tracers made by persons who allege receiving harassing telephone calls. The rules must address when a request for a tracer may be denied or delayed.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.069.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/442/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 442 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.07">
        <h1 class="shn">237.07 FILING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.07.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing of charges.</span></h2>
          <p>Every telephone company shall keep on file with the department a specific rate, toll, or charge for every kind of noncompetitive service and a price list for every kind of service subject to emerging competition, together with all rules and classifications used by it in the conduct of the telephone business, including limitations on liability. The filings are governed by chapter 13. When a company sells services subject to emerging competition on an individually priced basis, it shall file a statement of the charges to its customers with the commission and the department. The department shall require each telephone company to keep open for public inspection, at designated offices, so much of these rates, price lists, and rules as it deems necessary for the public information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.07.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Separate pricing.</span></h2>
          <p>When competitive services or service elements or services on an individually priced basis are sold in conjunction with noncompetitive services or service elements, the telephone company shall file or have on file with the commission and the department separate prices for its services subject to emerging competition and noncompetitive services or service elements. Telephone services or service elements must be offered on a nondiscriminatory basis.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/28">5290</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1915/0/152/#laws.0.5.0">1915 c 152 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/25/#laws.0.67.0">1971 c 25 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 74 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.071">
        <h1 class="shn">237.071 SPECIAL PRICING.</h1>
        <p>Except as prohibited by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.60#stat.237.60.3">237.60, subdivision 3</a>, prices unique to a particular customer or group of customers may be allowed for noncompetitive services and for services subject to emerging competition when differences in the cost of providing a service or a service element justifies a different price for a particular customer or group of customers. Individual pricing for services subject to emerging competition may be allowed when a uniform price should not be required because of market conditions. Unique or individual prices for services or service elements in effect before July 1, 1989, are deemed to have been approved under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.071.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 74 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.072">
        <h1 class="shn">237.072 LIMITATION ON RATE CHANGE.</h1>
        <p>(a) After December 15, 1997, the commission, notwithstanding any provision to the contrary, shall not allow an incumbent telephone company with more than 1,000,000 access lines in Minnesota to change its retail rates for telecommunications services without a determination of its revenue requirement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.075">237.075</a> unless the incumbent telephone company is regulated pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.773">237.773</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If, prior to December 15, 1997, the incumbent telephone company petitions the commission to become subject to an alternative regulation plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.773">237.773, paragraph (a</a>) shall not apply to the petitioning company until 270 days after the date of the filing of the petition.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.072.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/223/#laws.0.3.0">1997 c 223 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.3.0">1998 c 345 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.075">
        <h1 class="shn">237.075 RATE CHANGE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.075.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless the commission otherwise orders, no telephone company shall change a rate which has been duly established under this chapter, except upon 60 days' notice to the commission. The notice shall include statements of facts, expert opinions, substantiating documents, and exhibits, supporting the change requested, and state the change proposed to be made in the rates then in force and the time when the modified rates will go into effect. The filing telephone company shall give written notice, as approved by the commission, of the proposed change to the governing body of each municipality and county in the area affected. All proposed changes shall be shown by filing new schedules or shall be plainly indicated upon schedules on file and in force at the time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.075.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 74 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.075.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Suspension of proposed rate; hearing; final determination defined.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Whenever there is filed with the commission as provided in subdivision 1 a schedule modifying or resulting in a change in any rate then in force, the commission may suspend the operation of the schedule by filing with the schedule of rates and delivering to the affected telephone company a statement in writing of its reasons for the suspension at any time before the rates become effective. The suspension shall not be for a longer period than ten months beyond the initial filing date except as provided in paragraph (b). During the suspension the commission shall determine whether all questions of the reasonableness of the rates requested raised by persons deemed interested or by the department can be resolved to the satisfaction of the commission. If the commission finds that all significant issues raised have not been resolved to its satisfaction, or upon petition by ten percent of the affected customers or 250 affected customers, whichever is less, it shall refer the matter to the Office of Administrative Hearings with instructions for a public hearing as a contested case pursuant to chapter 14, except as otherwise provided in this section. The commission may order that the issues presented by the proposed rate changes be bifurcated into two separate hearings as follows: (1) determination of the telephone company's revenue requirements and (2) determination of the rate design. Upon issuance of both administrative law judge reports, the issues shall again be joined for consideration and final determination by the commission. All prehearing discovery activities of state agency intervenors shall be consolidated and conducted by the Department of Commerce. If the commission does not make a final determination concerning a schedule of rates within ten months after the initial filing date, the schedule shall be deemed to have been approved by the commission; except if a settlement has been submitted to and rejected by the commission, the schedule is deemed to have been approved 12 months after the initial filing.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commission finds that it has insufficient time during the suspension period to make a final determination of a case involving changes in general rates because of the need to make final determinations of other previously filed cases involving changes in general rates under this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216B.16">216B.16</a>, the commission may extend the suspension period to the extent necessary to allow itself 20 working days to make the final determination after it has made final determinations in the previously filed cases. An extension of the suspension period under this paragraph does not alter the setting of interim rates under subdivision 3.</p>
          <p>(c) For the purposes of this section, "final determination" means the initial decision of the commission and not any order which may be entered by the commission in response to a petition for rehearing or other further relief. The commission may further suspend rates until it determines all those petitions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.075.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Interim rate; refund.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any order of suspension of a proposed increase in rates, the commission shall order an interim rate schedule into effect not later than 60 days after the initial filing date. The commission shall order the interim rate schedule ex parte without a public hearing. Notwithstanding the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216.25">216.25</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.25">237.25</a>, no interim rate schedule ordered by the commission pursuant to this subdivision shall be subject to an application for a rehearing or an appeal to a court until the commission has rendered its final determination. Unless the commission finds that exigent circumstances exist, the interim rate schedule shall be calculated using the proposed test-year cost of capital, rate base, and expenses, except that it shall include: (1) a rate of return on common equity for the company equal to that authorized by the commission in the company's most recent rate proceeding; (2) rate base or expense items the same in nature and kind as those allowed by a currently effective order of the commission in the company's most recent rate proceeding; and (3) no change in the existing rate design, except for products and services offered by nonregulated competitors. In the case of a company which has not been subject to a prior commission determination or has not had a general rate adjustment in the preceding three years, the commission shall base the interim rate schedule on its most recent determination concerning a similar company.</p>
          <p>If, at the time of its final determination, the commission finds that the interim rates are in excess of the rates in the final determination, the commission shall order the company to refund the excess amount collected under the interim rate schedule, including interest on it which shall be at the rate of interest determined by the commission. The company shall commence distribution of the refund to its customers within 120 days of the final order, not subject to rehearing or appeal. If, at the time of its final determination, the commission finds that the interim rates are less than the rates in the final determination, the commission shall prescribe a method by which the company will recover the difference in revenues from the date of the final determination to the date the new rate schedules are put into effect.</p>
          <p>If the telephone company fails to make refunds within the period of time prescribed by the commission, the commission shall sue therefor and may recover on behalf of all persons entitled to a refund. In addition to the amount of the refund and interest due, the commission shall be entitled to recover reasonable attorney's fees, court costs and estimated cost of administering the distribution of the refund to persons entitled thereto. No suit under this subdivision shall be maintained unless instituted within two years after the end of the period of time prescribed by the commission for repayment of refunds. The commission shall not order an interim rate schedule in a general rate case into effect as provided by this subdivision until at least four months after it has made a final determination concerning any previously filed change of the rate schedule or the change has otherwise become effective under subdivision 2, unless:</p>
