          <p>(1) the telephone assistance program and the telecommunications access Minnesota program at the levels established by the commission under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.52#stat.237.52.2">237.52, subdivision 2</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.70">237.70</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the 911 emergency and public safety communications program at the levels appropriated by law to the commissioner of public safety and the commissioner of management and budget for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.11">403.11</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.113">403.113</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.27">403.27</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.30">403.30</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.31">403.31</a> for each fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(b) The recommendations must include any changes to Minnesota Statutes necessary to establish the procedures whereby each service provider, to the extent allowed under federal law, would collect and remit the fee proceeds to the commissioner of revenue. The commissioner of revenue would allocate the fee proceeds to the three funding areas in paragraph (a) and credit the allocations to the appropriate accounts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.491.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/136/#laws.10.1.0">2005 c 136 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.57.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.1.40.0">2014 c 222 art 1 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PERSONS WITH COMMUNICATION DISABILITIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.50">
        <h1 class="shn">237.50 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The terms used in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a> have the meanings given them in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Communication disability.</span></h2>
          <p>"Communication disability" means certified as having a hearing loss, speech disability, or physical disability that makes it difficult or impossible to use telecommunications services and equipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Deaf.</span></h2>
          <p>"Deaf" means a hearing loss of such severity that the person must depend primarily upon visual communication such as writing, lip reading, sign language, and gestures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.4b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4b.<span class="headnote">Deafblind.</span></h2>
          <p>"Deafblind" means any combination of vision and hearing loss which interferes with acquiring information from the environment to the extent that compensatory strategies and skills are necessary to access that or other information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.4c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4c.<span class="headnote">Discounted telecommunications or Internet services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Discounted telecommunications or Internet services" means private, nonprofit, and public programs intended to subsidize or reduce the monthly costs of telecommunications or Internet services for a person who meets a program's eligibility requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Fund.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fund" means the telecommunications access Minnesota fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.52">237.52</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Hard-of-hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>"Hard-of-hearing" means a hearing loss resulting in a functional limitation, but not to the extent that the person must depend primarily upon visual communication in all interactions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.6b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6b.<span class="headnote">Interconnectivity product.</span></h2>
          <p>"Interconnectivity product" means a device, accessory, or application for which the primary function is use with a telecommunications device. Interconnectivity product may include a cell phone amplifier, hearing aid streamer, Bluetooth-enabled device that connects to a wireless telecommunications device, advanced communications application for a smartphone, or other applicable technology.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.10a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10a.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications device.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications device" means a device that (1) allows a person with a communication disability to have access to telecommunications services as defined in subdivision 13, and (2) is specifically selected by the Department of Human Services for its capacity to allow persons with communication disabilities to use telecommunications services in a manner that is functionally equivalent to the ability of a person who does not have a communication disability. A telecommunications device may include a ring signaler, an amplified telephone, a hands-free telephone, a text telephone, a captioned telephone, a wireless device, a device that produces Braille output for use with a telephone, and any other device the Department of Human Services deems appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications Relay Services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications Relay Services" or "TRS" means the telecommunications transmission services required under Federal Communications Commission regulations at Code of Federal Regulations, title 47, sections 64.604 to 64.606. TRS allows a person who has a communication disability to use telecommunications services in a manner that is functionally equivalent to the ability of a person who does not have a communication disability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications" means the transmission, between or among points specified by the user, of information of the user's choosing, without change in the form or content of the information as sent and received.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.50.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Telecommunications services.</span></h2>
          <p>"Telecommunications services" means the offering of telecommunications for a fee directly to the public, or to such classes of users as to be effectively available to the public, regardless of the facilities used.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 308 s 1</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/621/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 621 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 272 s 2</a>-6,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 190 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/228/#laws.1.74.0">2004 c 228 art 1 s 74</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.1.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.5.1.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 5 s 1</a>-6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.51">
        <h1 class="shn">237.51 TELECOMMUNICATIONS ACCESS MINNESOTA PROGRAM ADMINISTRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of commerce shall:</p>
          <p>(1) administer through interagency agreement with the commissioner of human services a program to distribute telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products to eligible persons who have communication disabilities; and</p>
          <p>(2) contract with one or more qualified vendors that serve persons who have communication disabilities to provide telecommunications relay services.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) For purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51">237.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>, the Department of Commerce and any organization with which it contracts pursuant to this section or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.54#stat.237.54.2">237.54, subdivision 2</a>, are not telephone companies or telecommunications carriers as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01">237.01</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of commerce duties.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition to any duties specified elsewhere in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51">237.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>, the commissioner of commerce shall:</p>
          <p>(1) prepare the reports required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.55">237.55</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) administer the fund created in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.52">237.52</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) adopt rules under chapter 14 to implement the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Commissioner of human services duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to any duties specified elsewhere in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51">237.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>, the commissioner of human services shall:</p>
          <p>(1) define economic hardship, special needs, and household criteria so as to determine the priority of eligible applicants for initial distribution of devices and products and to determine circumstances necessitating provision of more than one telecommunications device per household;</p>
          <p>(2) establish a method to verify eligibility requirements;</p>
          <p>(3) establish specifications for telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products to be provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.53#stat.237.53.3">237.53, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) inform the public and specifically persons who have communication disabilities of the program;</p>
          <p>(5) provide devices and products based on the assessed need of eligible applicants; and</p>
          <p>(6) assist a person with completing an application for discounted telecommunications or Internet services.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may establish an advisory board to advise the department in carrying out the duties specified in this section and to advise the commissioner of commerce in carrying out duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.54">237.54</a>. If so established, the advisory board must include, at a minimum, the following persons:</p>
          <p>(1) at least one member who is deaf;</p>
          <p>(2) at least one member who has a speech disability;</p>
          <p>(3) at least one member who has a physical disability that makes it difficult or impossible for the person to access telecommunications services; and</p>
          <p>(4) at least one member who is hard-of-hearing.</p>
          <p>(c) The membership terms, compensation, and removal of members and the filling of membership vacancies are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059">15.059</a>. Advisory board meetings shall be held at the discretion of the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.51.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/186/#laws.0.15.0">1987 c 186 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 308 s 2</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/621/#laws.0.3.0">1988 c 621 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/571/#laws.0.41.0">1990 c 571 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/598/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 598 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/430/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 430 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/518/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 518 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 272 s 7</a>-11,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 190 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.70.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/149/#laws.0.1.0">1999 c 149 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.60.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 60</a>-62; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/329/#laws.0.2.0">2002 c 329 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.2.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.5.7.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 5 s 7</a>,8</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.52">
        <h1 class="shn">237.52 TELECOMMUNICATIONS ACCESS MINNESOTA FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fund established.</span></h2>
          <p>A telecommunications access Minnesota fund is established as an account in the state treasury. Earnings, such as interest, dividends, and any other earnings arising from fund assets, must be credited to the fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of commerce, the commissioner of employment and economic development, and the commissioner of human services shall annually recommend to the Public Utilities Commission an adequate and appropriate surcharge and budget to implement sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a>, respectively. The maximum annual budget for section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062</a> must not exceed $100,000 and for section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a> must not exceed $300,000. The Public Utilities Commission shall review the budgets for reasonableness and may modify the budget to the extent it is unreasonable. The commission shall annually determine the funding mechanism to be used within 60 days of receipt of the recommendation of the departments and shall order the imposition of surcharges effective on the earliest practicable date. The commission shall establish a monthly charge no greater than 20 cents for each customer access line, including trunk equivalents as designated by the commission pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.11#stat.403.11.1">403.11, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If the fund balance falls below a level capable of fully supporting all programs eligible under subdivision 5 and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a>, expenditures under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a> shall be reduced on a pro rata basis and expenditures under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.53">237.53</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.54">237.54</a> shall be fully funded. Expenditures under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a> shall resume at fully funded levels when the commissioner of commerce determines there is a sufficient fund balance to fully fund those expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Collection.</span></h2>
          <p>Every provider of services capable of originating a TRS call, including cellular communications and other nonwire access services, in this state shall, except as provided in subdivision 3a, collect the charges established by the commission under subdivision 2 and transfer amounts collected to the commissioner of public safety in the same manner as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.11#stat.403.11.1">403.11, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (d). The commissioner of public safety must deposit the receipts in the fund established in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Fee for prepaid wireless telecommunications service.</span></h2>
          <p>The fee established in subdivision 2 does not apply to prepaid wireless telecommunications services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.02#stat.403.02.17d">403.02, subdivision 17d</a>, which are instead subject to the prepaid wireless telecommunications access Minnesota fee established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.161">403.161</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b). Collection, remittance, and deposit of prepaid wireless telecommunications access Minnesota fees are governed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.161">403.161</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/403.162">403.162</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>Money in the fund is appropriated to the commissioner of commerce to implement sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51">237.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>, to the commissioner of employment and economic development to implement section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/248.062">248.062</a>, and to the commissioner of human services to implement section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256C.30">256C.30</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.52.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Money in the fund may only be used for:</p>
          <p>(1) expenses of the Department of Commerce, including personnel cost, public relations, advisory board members' expenses, preparation of reports, and other reasonable expenses not to exceed ten percent of total program expenditures;</p>
          <p>(2) reimbursing the commissioner of human services for purchases made or services provided pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.53">237.53</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) contracting for the provision of TRS required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.54">237.54</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) All costs directly associated with the establishment of the program, the purchase and distribution of telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products, and the provision of TRS are either reimbursable or directly payable from the fund after authorization by the commissioner of commerce. The commissioner of commerce shall contract with one or more TRS providers to indemnify the telecommunications service providers for any fines imposed by the Federal Communications Commission related to the failure of the relay service to comply with federal service standards. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.41">16A.41</a>, the commissioner may advance money to the TRS providers if the providers establish to the commissioner's satisfaction that the advance payment is necessary for the provision of the service. The advance payment may be used only for working capital reserve for the operation of the service. The advance payment must be offset or repaid by the end of the contract fiscal year together with interest accrued from the date of payment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.52.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 308 s 3</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/621/#laws.0.4.0">1988 c 621 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/518/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 518 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.12.0">1993 c 272 s 12</a>,13,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.5.0">1995 c 190 s 5</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/201/#laws.0.1.0">1995 c 201 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.63.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 63</a>-65; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/329/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 329 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.67.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/81/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 81 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.3.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.13.3.0">2013 c 143 art 13 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.5.9.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 5 s 9</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.53">
        <h1 class="shn">237.53 TELECOMMUNICATIONS DEVICES AND INTERCONNECTIVITY PRODUCTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>A person applying for a telecommunications device or interconnectivity product under this section must apply to the program administrator on a form prescribed by the Department of Human Services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>To be eligible to obtain a telecommunications device or interconnectivity product under this section, a person must:</p>
          <p>(1) be able to benefit from and use the equipment for its intended purpose;</p>
          <p>(2) have a communication disability;</p>
          <p>(3) be a resident of the state;</p>
          <p>(4) be a resident in a household that has a median income at or below the applicable median household income in the state, except a person who is deafblind applying for a Braille device may reside in a household that has a median income no more than 150 percent of the applicable median household income in the state; and</p>
          <p>(5) be a resident in a household that has telecommunications service or that has made application for service and has been assigned a telephone number; or a resident in a residential care facility, such as a nursing home or group home where telecommunications service is not included as part of overall service provision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Assessment of needs.</span></h2>
          <p>After a person is determined to be eligible for the program, the commissioner of human services shall assess the person's telecommunications needs to determine (1) the type of telecommunications device that provides the person with functionally equivalent access to telecommunications services, and (2) appropriate interconnectivity products for the person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Distribution.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall (1) purchase a sufficient number of telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products so that each eligible household receives appropriate devices and products as determined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51#stat.237.51.5a">237.51, subdivision 5a</a>, and (2) distribute the devices and products to eligible households free of charge.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Training; information; maintenance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall maintain the telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products until the warranty period expires, and provide training, without charge, to first-time users of the devices and products. The commissioner shall provide information about assistive communications devices and products that may benefit a program participant and about where a person may obtain or purchase assistive communications devices and products. Assistive communications devices and products include a pocket talker for a person who is hard-of-hearing, a communication board for a person with a speech disability, a one-to-one video communication application for a person who is deaf, and other devices and products designed to facilitate effective communication for a person with a communication disability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.4.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Ownership.</span></h2>
          <p>Telecommunications devices and interconnectivity products purchased pursuant to subdivision 3, clause (1), are the property of the state of Minnesota. Policies and procedures for the return of distributed devices and products shall be determined by the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>The telecommunications devices distributed under this section must comply with the electronic industries alliance standards and be approved by the Federal Communications Commission. The commissioner of human services must provide each eligible person a choice of several models of devices, the retail value of which may not exceed $600 for a text telephone, and a retail value of $7,000 for a Braille device, or an amount authorized by the Department of Human Services for all other telecommunications devices, auxiliary equipment, and interconnectivity products it deems cost-effective and appropriate to distribute according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.51">237.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.56">237.56</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/621/#laws.0.19.0">1988 c 621 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.53.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Discounted telecommunications or Internet services assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services shall assist a person who is applying for telecommunication devices and products in applying for discounted telecommunications or Internet services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.53.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 308 s 4</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/621/#laws.0.5.0">1988 c 621 s 5</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.17.0">1993 c 272 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.8.0">1995 c 190 s 8</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/201/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 201 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.4.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.5.10.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 5 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.54">
        <h1 class="shn">237.54 TELECOMMUNICATIONS RELAY SERVICES (TRS).</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.54.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 190 s 17</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.54.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Operation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of commerce shall contract with one or more qualified vendors for the provision of Telecommunications Relay Services (TRS).</p>
          <p>(b) The TRS providers shall operate the relay service within the state of Minnesota. The TRS providers shall comply with all current and subsequent Federal Communications Commission regulations at Code of Federal Regulations, title 47, sections 64.601 to 64.606, and shall inform persons who have communication disabilities and the public of the availability and use of TRS.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 308 s 5</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.14.0">1993 c 272 s 14</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.12.0">1995 c 190 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.66.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/329/#laws.0.4.0">2002 c 329 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.5.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.55">
        <h1 class="shn">237.55 ANNUAL REPORT ON TELECOMMUNICATIONS ACCESS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of commerce must prepare a report for presentation to the Public Utilities Commission by March 31 each year. Each report must review the accessibility of telecommunications services to persons who have communication disabilities, describe services provided, account for annual revenues and expenditures, and include anticipated future program operations.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.6.0">1987 c 308 s 6</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.15.0">1993 c 272 s 15</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/190/#laws.0.13.0">1995 c 190 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.67.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.6.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.12.62.0">2023 c 60 art 12 s 62</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.56">
        <h1 class="shn">237.56 ADEQUATE SERVICE ENFORCEMENT.</h1>
        <p>The services required to be provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.55">237.55</a> may be enforced under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a> upon a complaint of at least two persons within the service area of any one telecommunications service provider, provided that if only one person within the service area of a company is receiving service under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.50">237.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.55">237.55</a>, the Public Utilities Commission may proceed upon a complaint from that person.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/308/#laws.0.7.0">1987 c 308 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/272/#laws.0.17.0">1993 c 272 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.10.7.0">2012 c 216 art 10 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMPETITIVE SERVICES; INCENTIVES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.57">
        <h1 class="shn">237.57 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The terms used in this chapter have the meanings given them in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Competitive service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Competitive service" means a service that has been determined to be subject to effective competition or emerging competition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effective competition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Effective competition" exists when the criteria of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.59#stat.237.59.5">237.59, subdivision 5</a>, have been satisfied for a service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Emerging competition.</span></h2>
          <p>A service will be regulated under "emerging competition" provisions when the criteria of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.59#stat.237.59.5">237.59, subdivision 5</a>, have not been satisfied, but there is a trend toward effective competition, or if it is a new service offered for the first time after August 1, 1994, that is not integrally related to the provision of adequate telephone service or access to the telephone network or to the privacy, health, or safety of the company's customers, whether or not it meets the criteria of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.59#stat.237.59.5">237.59, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Local access and transport area.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local access and transport area" (LATA) means a geographical area designated by the Modification of Final Judgment in <i>U.S. v. Western Electric Co., Inc.</i>, 552 F. Supp. 131 (D.D.C. 1982).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.57.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Noncompetitive service.</span></h2>
          <p>"Noncompetitive service" means a service that has not been classified as competitive by the commission.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.57.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 340 s 1</a>,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.7.0">1989 c 74 s 7</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/534/#laws.1.2.0">1994 c 534 art 1 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.5799"><b>237.5799</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.58"><b>237.58</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.59">
        <h1 class="shn">237.59 CLASSIFICATION OF COMPETITIVE SERVICE; HEARING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Emerging competitive service.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The following services provided by the telephone company are subject to emerging competition unless and until reclassified as noncompetitive or subject to effective competition under this section:</p>
          <p>(1) apartment door answering services;</p>
          <p>(2) automatic call distribution;</p>
          <p>(3) billing and collection services;</p>
          <p>(4) call waiting, call forwarding, and three-way calling services for businesses with three or more lines;</p>
          <p>(5) central office-based pricing packages providing switched business access lines which substitute for private branch exchange systems which may or may not share intelligence with customer premises equipment;</p>
          <p>(6) command link-type services for network reconfiguring to rearrange cross-connections between channel services;</p>
          <p>(7) custom network services and special assemblies;</p>
          <p>(8) Digicom switchnet services for full duplex, synchronous, information transport;</p>
          <p>(9) direct customer access services for telephone number information;</p>
          <p>(10) teleconferencing services;</p>
          <p>(11) inter-LATA and intra-LATA message toll service;</p>
          <p>(12) inter-LATA and intra-LATA private line services;</p>
          <p>(13) inter-LATA and intra-LATA wide area telephone service;</p>
          <p>(14) mobile radio services;</p>
          <p>(15) operator services, excluding local operator services;</p>
          <p>(16) public pay telephone services, excluding charges for access to the central office;</p>
          <p>(17) special construction of facilities;</p>
          <p>(18) systems for automatic dialing; and</p>
          <p>(19) versanet-type service access line involving continuous monitoring and transmission of data from customer's premises to the central office.</p>
          <p>(b) A service classified as subject to emerging competition before June 1, 1994, retains that classification unless and until it is reclassified pursuant to subdivision 3 or 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">CLASS service.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the terms of subdivision 1, paragraph (b), CLASS services may be classified as competitive services only when so classified according to subdivision 3 or 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Petition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A telephone company, or the commission on its own motion, may petition to have a service of that telephone company classified as subject to effective competition or emerging competition. The petition must be served on the commission, the department, the Office of the Attorney General, and any other person designated by the commission. The petition must contain at least:</p>
          <p>(1) a list of the known alternative providers of the service available to the company's customers; and</p>
          <p>(2) a description of affiliate relationships with any other provider of the service in the company's market.</p>
          <p>(b) At the time the company first offers a service, it shall also file a petition with the commission for a determination as to how the service should be classified. In the event that no interested party or the commission objects to the company's proposed classification within 20 days of the filing of the petition, the company's proposed classification of the service is deemed approved. If an objection is filed, the commission shall determine the appropriate classification after a hearing conducted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.61">237.61</a>. In either event, the company may offer the new service to its customers ten days after the company files the price list and incremental cost study as provided in Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7811.2210">7811.2210</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/7812.2210">7812.2210</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) A new service may be classified as subject to effective competition or emerging competition pursuant to the criteria set forth in subdivision 5. A new service must be regulated under the emerging competition provisions if it is not integrally related to the provision of adequate local service or access to the telephone network or to the privacy, health, or safety of the company's customers, whether or not it meets the criteria set forth in subdivision 5.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Expedited proceeding.</span></h2>
          <p>An interested party wishing to contest the change of classification of a service must file an objection with the commission within 20 days after the filing of the petition. If no party files an objection, the service must be reclassified in accordance with the petition. If a petition is contested, a telephone company that is the subject of a petition under subdivision 2 may request that the commission determine the classification of the service through an expedited proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.61">237.61</a> or a contested case hearing. If an expedited proceeding is requested, the commission must provide interested persons an opportunity to comment on the appropriateness of the process and the merits of the petition.</p>
          <p>When an expedited proceeding is requested, the commission shall make a final determination within 60 days of the date on which all required information required under subdivision 2 is filed, unless during the 60 days the commission finds that a material issue of fact is in dispute, in which case it shall order that a contested case hearing be conducted to evaluate the petition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contested case hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>If a contested case hearing is held under this section, the commission shall make a final determination on the petition within eight months from the date the petitioning party requests a contested case hearing or from the date the commission orders a contested case hearing under subdivision 3. When a contested case hearing is requested in the petition or when the commission acts on its own motion, this deadline may be extended for no more than 60 days by agreement of all parties or by order of the commission if the commission finds that the case cannot be completed within the required time and that without an extension there is substantial probability that the public interest will be harmed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a proposed classification is objected to pursuant to subdivision 2, paragraph (b), on the basis that the service does not meet the criteria of this subdivision, the commission shall consider, in determining whether a service is subject to either effective competition or emerging competition from available alternative service providers, the following factors:</p>
          <p>(1) the number and sizes of alternative providers of service and affiliation to other providers;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which services are available from alternative providers in the relevant market;</p>
          <p>(3) the ability of alternative providers to make functionally equivalent or substitute services readily available at competitive rates, terms, and conditions of service;</p>
          <p>(4) the market share, the ability of the market to hold prices close to cost, and other economic measures of market power; and</p>
          <p>(5) the necessity of the service to the well-being of the customer.</p>
          <p>(b) In order for the commission to find a service subject to effective competition alternative services must be available to over 50 percent of the company's customers for that service.</p>
          <p>(c) In order for the commission to find a service subject to emerging competition alternative services must be available to over 20 percent of the company's customers for that service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof.</span></h2>
          <p>The classification of a service may not be changed so as to result in lessened regulation unless it is demonstrated by a preponderance of the evidence that the criteria of subdivision 5 have been met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/268/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 268 s 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Interim relief.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company that has a petition pending before the commission under this section to declare a service competitive may decrease its price for that service without notice while the commission considers the petition. A company must provide an incremental cost study if requested by the commission. The commission shall suspend a company's right under this subdivision to decrease rates if, after an expedited hearing conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.61">237.61</a>, the commission finds that the service is being priced below cost, or that the company has within the previous 12 months charged customers interim rates under this subdivision for the same service, and that service was determined by the commission to be noncompetitive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Reporting requirements; exception.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company that offers only competitive services is not subject to the accounting and reporting requirements of this chapter unless otherwise ordered by the commission for good cause. A telephone company that offers both competitive and noncompetitive services is not subject to the reporting requirements with regard to its effective competition services unless otherwise ordered by the commission for good cause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.59.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Regulation reinstated.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission, on its own motion or upon complaint, shall reclassify a service as noncompetitive or as subject to emerging competition and reinstate, in whole or in part, rate regulation of the service if, after notice and hearing, the commission finds either:</p>
          <p>(1) that the competitive market for that service, on review of the criteria found in subdivision 5, has failed so that rate regulation of that service is necessary to protect the interest of consumers, that it has considered the alternatives to rate regulation, and that the benefits of rate regulation outweigh the burdens of rate regulation; or</p>
          <p>(2) that unreasonable discrimination has occurred between different areas of the state.</p>
          <p>(b) In any proceeding to reclassify a service the person initiating the complaint has the burden of proving that the existing classification is inappropriate, except the telephone company providing the service has the burden of proving that the classification is appropriate when the proceeding is commenced by the commission on its own motion or when the complainant is the department or the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.59.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 340 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.9.0">1989 c 74 s 9</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/534/#laws.1.5.0">1994 c 534 art 1 s 5</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.68.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.46.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.60">
        <h1 class="shn">237.60 DISCRIMINATORY PRACTICES; SERVICE COSTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.60.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.60.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.60.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Discrimination.</span></h2>
          <p>No telephone company shall offer telecommunications service within the state upon terms or rates that are unreasonably discriminatory. No telephone company shall unreasonably limit its service offerings to particular geographic areas unless facilities necessary for the service are not available and cannot be made available at reasonable costs. The rates of a telephone company must be the same in all geographic locations of the state unless for good cause the commission approves different rates. A company that offers long-distance services shall charge uniform rates and charges on all long-distance routes and in all geographic areas in the state where it offers the services. However, a company may offer or provide volume discounts in connection with intrastate long-distance services and may pass through any state, municipal, or local taxes in the specific geographic areas from which the taxes originate. Nothing in this subdivision authorizes a telephone company to provide service outside of its authorized service area except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.60.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cost of service.</span></h2>
          <p>Prices or rates charged for competitive services must cover the incremental costs of providing the service. If a telephone company provides both local service and long-distance services, that company shall, in determining the cost of the long-distance service, include at least the same level of contribution to common and joint costs as is contained in the access charges to other telephone companies. The company may do so on an aggregate basis, instead of on a time or mileage band basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.60.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.60.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 340 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.13.0">1989 c 74 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/493/#laws.0.7.0">1992 c 493 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.1.28.0">1994 c 465 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/534/#laws.1.10.0">1994 c 534 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.61">
        <h1 class="shn">237.61 EXPEDITED PROCEEDING.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding chapter 14, the commission may conduct an expedited proceeding when authorized under this chapter. In an expedited proceeding, the commission shall give prior notice to interested persons and provide them with an opportunity to present statements of fact and argument and to reply, either orally or in writing or both. In an expedited proceeding, the pleadings must be verified, and oral statements of fact must be made under oath or affirmation. The commission shall make a decision in an expedited proceeding based on the record.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.61.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.5.0">1987 c 340 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.62"><b>237.62</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.625"><b>237.625</b> [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.626">
        <h1 class="shn">237.626 PROMOTION ACTIVITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.626.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Promotions.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company or telecommunications carrier may promote the use of its services by offering a waiver of part or all of a recurring or a nonrecurring charge, a redemption coupon, or a premium with the purchase of a service. Section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.09">237.09</a> does not apply to promotions under this section, but the customer group to which the promotion is available must be based on reasonable distinctions among customers. The service being promoted must have a price that is above the incremental cost of the service, including amortized cost of the promotion. A promotion may take effect the day after the notice is filed with the commission. The notice must identify customers to whom the promotion is available.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.626.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bundled service.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A telephone company or telecommunications carrier may offer telecommunications services subject to the regulatory jurisdiction of the commission as part of a package of services that may include goods and services other than those subject to the commission's regulatory jurisdiction. Subject to the requirements of this chapter and the associated rules and orders of the commission applicable to those regulated services, a telephone company may establish the prices, terms, and conditions of a package of services, except that:</p>
          <p>(1) each telecommunications service subject to the regulatory jurisdiction of the commission must be available to customers on a stand-alone basis; and</p>
          <p>(2) at the time the packaged offering is introduced or at the time the packaged price is subsequently changed, the packaged rate or price may not exceed the sum of the unpackaged rates or prices for the individual service elements or services.</p>
          <p>(b) Nothing in this subdivision is intended to extend or diminish the regulatory authority of the commission or the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.626.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Promotions available for resale.</span></h2>
          <p>Any promotional offering lasting more than 90 days and filed with the commission under subdivision 1 must be available to qualifying carriers for resale. A qualifying carrier must hold a certificate of authority from the commission and must have an approved interconnection agreement with the company offering the promotion, the terms of which include language governing the resale of services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.626.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/493/#laws.0.8.0">1992 c 493 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.1.0">2003 c 97 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/57/#laws.0.2.0">2009 c 57 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.63"><b>237.63</b> MS 2002 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/224/#laws.0.7.0">1999 c 224 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/261/#laws.6.3.0">2004 c 261 art 6 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.64">
        <h1 class="shn">237.64 REGISTRATION; BOND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.64.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Registration.</span></h2>
          <p>A person, firm, or corporation seeking to become a telephone company, as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01#stat.237.01.7">237.01, subdivision 7</a>, and not required to be certified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a>, shall register with the department and the commission 90 days before beginning operation in the state. The commission may review the proposed rates and services and the financial conditions of the telephone company and may, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>, investigate any other matter it considers appropriate to protect the public interest. A telephone company that has been authorized by the commission to provide telephone services in this state prior to August 1, 1987, is not required to register under this subdivision. A person, firm, or corporation seeking to offer a noncompetitive service to the public must obtain authority from the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.64.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bond.</span></h2>
          <p>Telephone companies that have registered under subdivision 1 shall maintain a bond if the company requires advance payments or deposits from its customers, unless waived by the commission. The bond must be issued by a surety company admitted to do business in this state in the principal sum of all deposits and advance payments to be held by the company. The department shall determine the amount of the bond and may require the company to supply information to determine the appropriate amount of the bond. The bond must be in favor of the state for the benefit of any customer who suffers the loss of a deposit or advance payment due to insolvency, cessation of business, or failure to return any unused portion of the deposit or advance payment. The bond must be filed with the department.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.64.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.8.0">1987 c 340 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/74/#laws.0.23.0">1989 c 74 s 23</a>,24</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.237.65"><b>237.65</b> MS 2002 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/224/#laws.0.7.0">1999 c 224 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/261/#laws.6.3.0">2004 c 261 art 6 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.66">
        <h1 class="shn">237.66 DISCLOSURE OF LOCAL SERVICE OPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice to local residential customers.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company, when a residential customer initially requests service or requests a change of service, and annually in the form of a bill insert, shall advise each residential customer of the price of all service options available to that customer. The requirement of an annual notice through a bill insert does not apply to long-distance service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Notice to customer; right to require prior authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>Each residential and commercial telecommunications carrier customer may elect to require that the telephone company serving the customer receive authorization from the customer before a request to serve that customer from a different intrastate telecommunications carrier than the carrier currently serving the customer is processed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.16.0">1998 c 345 s 16</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Timing of notice; new customer.</span></h2>
          <p>For new installations, a telephone company shall notify a residential or commercial customer of the right described in subdivision 1a when the customer initially requests intraexchange service. Any customer notification of the rights set forth in this section shall be provided utilizing uniform, competitively neutral language and the form, content, and style of the authorization shall be consistent with federal law and regulation and shall use language provided and approved by the public utilities commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.1d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1d.<span class="headnote">Change of election.</span></h2>
          <p>A customer may change the election under subdivision 1a at any time by notifying the telephone company of that decision. No separate charge may be imposed on the customer for electing to exercise the right described in subdivision 1a or to change that election, but a telephone company may recover in rates the reasonable costs of administering the election.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Filing; exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Copies of both the written notices and information provided to customer service representatives concerning the disclosure required under subdivision 1 must be filed once every 12 months with the commission and the department. Independent telephone companies, municipalities, and cooperative telephone associations are exempt from the requirements of this subdivision unless otherwise ordered by the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Call blocking.</span></h2>
          <p>A telephone company, when a residential customer initially requests service, shall advise each residential customer of the availability of all blocking options including 900 number blocking and international long-distance blocking.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>If, after an expedited procedure conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.61">237.61</a>, the commission finds that a telephone company is failing to provide disclosure as required under subdivision 1, or the notification required under subdivision 1c, it shall order the company to take corrective action as necessary.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/340/#laws.0.10.0">1987 c 340 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/449/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 449 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/340/#laws.0.2.0">1996 c 340 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/68/#laws.0.4.0">1997 c 68 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.6.0">1998 c 345 s 6</a>-9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.661">
        <h1 class="shn">237.661 ANTISLAMMING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.661.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Antislamming duties of local telephone company.</span></h2>
          <p>If a customer has elected to exercise the right described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.66#stat.237.66.1a">237.66, subdivision 1a</a>, the telephone company serving the customer shall not process a request to serve the customer by another telecommunications carrier without prior authorization from the customer. If a customer has not elected to exercise the right described in that subdivision, the company may process a request to serve the customer by another telecommunications carrier.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.661.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Antislamming duties of soliciting carrier.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A telecommunications carrier may request that the telephone company serving a customer process a change in that customer's long-distance provider, if the customer has authorized the change either orally or in writing signed by the customer. Prior to requesting a change in a customer's long-distance service provider, the carrier must confirm:</p>
          <p>(1) the customer's identity with information unique to the customer, unless the customer refused to provide identifying information, then that fact should be noted;</p>
          <p>(2) that the customer has been informed of the offering made by the carrier;</p>
          <p>(3) that the customer understands that the customer is being requested to change telecommunication carriers;</p>
          <p>(4) that the customer has the authority to authorize the change; and</p>
          <p>(5) that the customer agrees to the change.</p>
          <p>(b) After requesting the change in long-distance service provider, the carrier must:</p>
          <p>(1) notify the customer in writing that the request has been processed; and</p>
          <p>(2) be able to produce, upon complaint by the customer, evidence that the carrier verified the authorization by the customer to change the customer's long-distance service provider. If the carrier used a negative checkoff verification procedure as defined in subdivision 4, paragraph (c), the evidence must include a tape recording of the initial oral authorization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.661.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Penalty for slamming.</span></h2>
          <p>If the carrier is not able to present, upon complaint by the customer, evidence that complies with subdivision 2, paragraph (b), clause (2), the change to the service of the carrier is deemed to be unauthorized from the date the carrier requested the change. In that event, the carrier shall:</p>
          <p>(1) bear all costs of immediately returning the customer to the service of the customer's original service provider; and</p>
          <p>(2) bear all costs of serving that customer during the period of unauthorized service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.661.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Verification procedures; evidence of authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Customer authorization for a change in the customer's long-distance service provider may be verified using a verification procedure that complies with federal law or regulation. Except as provided in paragraph (b), the requirement that the carrier be able to produce evidence of customer authorization is satisfied if the carrier uses a federally authorized verification procedure.</p>
          <p>(b) If federal law or regulation authorizes a carrier to use a negative checkoff verification procedure, and the carrier does so, the carrier must be able to produce a tape recording of the initial oral authorization by the customer to change long-distance service providers as evidence of the authorization. The initial oral authorization must include confirmation of the items listed in subdivision 2, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) "Negative checkoff" means a verification procedure that consists of:</p>
          <p>(1) an initial oral authorization by the customer to change long-distance service providers; and</p>
          <p>(2) a mailing to the customer by the soliciting telecommunications carrier regarding the change in service providers that informs the customer that if the customer fails to cancel the change in service providers, the change will be deemed authorized and verified.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.661.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.10.0">1998 c 345 s 10</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.662">
        <h1 class="shn">237.662 NOTICE AND DISCLOSURE REQUIREMENTS OF LONG-DISTANCE PROVIDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.662.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Information required.</span></h2>
          <p>When contacted by a customer regarding the purchase of long-distance telecommunications services, or when soliciting customers via mail or telephone, a provider of long-distance services shall provide the customer with the following information, if the service is being offered to the customer, about the service offering either orally or in writing:</p>
          <p>(1) the price or range of prices of interstate message toll service accessed by dialing "1+" or "10-xxx", including any difference in prices for evening, night, or weekend calls;</p>
          <p>(2) the price or range of prices of intrastate interLATA message toll service accessed by dialing "1+" or "10-xxx", including any difference in prices for evening, night, or weekend calls;</p>
          <p>(3) the price or range of prices of intrastate intraLATA message toll service accessed by dialing "1+" or "10-xxx", including any difference in prices for evening, night, or weekend;</p>
          <p>(4) any minimum volume requirements, fixed flat fees, service charges, surcharges, termination charges or other non-service-specific charges, including the fact that the provider of local service may charge a onetime fee for changing carriers; and</p>
          <p>(5) any special promotional rate or promotional offering related to the services or prices described in clauses (1) to (4) above, including any limitations or restrictions on the promotional rates or offerings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.662.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Price, terms, and restrictions in writing.</span></h2>
          <p>If a customer agrees to purchase telecommunications services from the provider of long-distance services on a presubscription basis, the provider shall send the customer written information regarding services subscribed to, containing:</p>
          <p>(1) the information regarding prices and charges described in subdivision 1, clauses (1) to (5);</p>
          <p>(2) the price for calls placed with a calling card issued to the customer by the provider and any surcharge for placing calls with a calling card;</p>
          <p>(3) the price for calls charged to the customer when a personal "1-800" number for long-distance services issued to the customer by the provider is used; and</p>
          <p>(4) the price of directory assistance calls.</p>
          <p>This written information must be sent to the customer within seven business days from the date of the verification of the customer's authorization, unless federal law or regulation requires notice to be sent by an earlier date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.662.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Filed tariff no defense.</span></h2>
          <p>That a telecommunications carrier has intrastate tariffs or price lists for the services listed in subdivisions 1 and 2 on file with the Public Utilities Commission or Department of Commerce is not a defense to any action brought for failure to disclose intrastate prices for which disclosure is required under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.662.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.11.0">1998 c 345 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.77.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 77</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.663">
        <h1 class="shn">237.663 LOADING.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b) or (c), a telephone company or telecommunications carrier providing local service shall not charge a telephone service subscriber, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/325F.692">325F.692</a>, for a telephone or telecommunications service that is not required by the commission to be offered and for which the subscriber did not explicitly contract.</p>
        <p>(b) If a charge is assessed on a per-use basis for a service described in paragraph (a), the charge must be applied as a credit to the subscriber's next monthly bill, if the subscriber notifies the telephone company or telecommunications carrier that the subscriber did not utilize the service or did not authorize the utilization of the service.</p>
        <p>(c) A telephone company or telecommunications carrier that receives a notification from a telephone service subscriber under paragraph (b) shall inform the subscriber of the ability to block the services from future use by the subscriber, and shall block the services from future use by the subscriber, if the subscriber so requests. If a subscriber requests that the carrier or company not block the service or later requests to have the block lifted, the subscriber shall be responsible for charges caused by the future utilization of that service. The carrier or company may not charge a recurring fee for blocking the service.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.663.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/345/#laws.0.12.0">1998 c 345 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.665">
        <h1 class="shn">237.665 PROHIBITION AGAINST BILLING FOR UNAUTHORIZED CHARGES.</h1>
        <p>(a) A telephone company or telecommunications carrier providing local service shall not include on a customer's bill a charge for goods or services on behalf of a third-party service provider unless the third-party service provider has obtained the customer's prior express authorization to include such charges on the customer's bill.</p>
