          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Plan duration.</span></h2>
          <p>An alternative regulation plan approved by the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.764">237.764</a> must remain in force as approved for the term specified in the plan, which must be for no less than three years. Except as otherwise provided in this section, within six months prior to the termination of the plan the company shall give notice that it will propose a new plan, extend an existing plan, or revert to rate of return regulation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.766.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">New plan.</span></h2>
          <p>A new plan proposed by a company must be reviewed by the commission and, with the consent of the company, revised or approved consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.774">237.774</a>, except that the justification of earnings levels in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.764#stat.237.764.1">237.764, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), if required, and the provisions prohibiting rate increases at the initiation of or during the first three years of a plan contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.762">237.762</a>, shall not apply to a new plan. Any new plan must be approved by the commission and shall contain a mechanism under which a telephone company may reduce the rates for price-regulated services below the initial rates or prices or increase the rates or prices during the term of the plan. The plan must specify the reports required of the telephone company for review of the plan and specify that the telephone company shall maintain records in sufficient detail to facilitate the review. A new plan is not an extension, which must be made pursuant to subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.766.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Plan extension.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding the provisions of its plan, a telephone company operating under a plan as of May 20, 2004, may elect to extend that plan for up to three years from the expiration date of the plan or until December 31, 2007, whichever is earlier. The election is effective upon notification to customers, the commission, the department, and the Office of the Attorney General. A telephone company must provide notification of its election within 30 days of May 20, 2004, or within six months of the expiration of its current or expired plan, whichever is later. Once a telephone company has elected to exercise the option provided under this subdivision, the company may elect at any time to terminate the plan by notifying customers, the commission, the department, and the Office of the Attorney General, in writing, six months prior to the termination date. Upon termination of a plan, the company shall be regulated as provided in this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) A telephone company may elect to extend a plan entered into after May 20, 2004, in lieu of proposing a new plan only if the company is in substantial compliance with the plan's service quality provisions and has met its infrastructure obligations under the plan. If the company elects to extend a plan, the rates for price-regulated services shall be capped at the rate levels in effect at the time the extension commences, provided, however, exceptions to a price cap contained in the plan being extended may remain in force. Unless otherwise specified in the plan, all other provisions of the plan shall continue in effect throughout the extension period. A plan may not be extended for less than one year or more than three years, and may only be extended once.</p>
          <p>(c) The Department of Commerce or the Office of the Attorney General may file an objection to the extension with the commission if the company is not in substantial compliance with the service quality provisions of its plan or has not met its infrastructure obligations under the plan. An objection must be filed within 45 days of the company's notice of its intention to extend the plan.</p>
          <p>(d) If an objection is filed by the Department of Commerce or the Office of the Attorney General, the commission may hold a hearing on the issues raised in the objection. The hearings shall be completed within 30 days of the deadline for filing the objections. If the commission finds that the issues raised in the objection are valid, it may reject the extension. If the commission finds that the issues raised in the objection are not valid, it shall approve the extension. The commission shall issue its decision within 15 days of the completion of the hearings concerning the objection.</p>
          <p>(e) If the Department of Commerce or the Office of the Attorney General does not file an objection, the commission shall approve the extension within 60 days of the company's filing of its notice of its intention to extend the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.766.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Joining an existing plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A telephone company may elect to opt into another company's plan if:</p>
          <p>(1) the chosen plan is from a company that is larger than the electing company; or</p>
          <p>(2) the chosen plan is from an affiliated company; and</p>
          <p>(3) the plan is currently in effect.</p>
          <p>(b) A telephone company electing to enter an existing plan in lieu of proposing a new plan must operate under the terms of that plan for at least three years. If the original term of the existing plan was longer than three years, then the adopting company must operate under the plan for that longer period.</p>
          <p>(c) A telephone company that desires to adopt an existing plan must give notice to the commission at least 90 days prior to the proposed effective date of the adoption and to its customers at least 60 days prior to the proposed effective date.</p>
          <p>(d) The Department of Commerce or the Office of the Attorney General may file an objection to a telephone company that has previously operated under a plan from electing to opt into the plan of another company if the electing company is not in substantial compliance with the service quality provisions or has not met the infrastructure obligations of its plan.</p>
          <p>(e) If a telephone company has not previously operated under an alternative regulation plan, the rates for its price-regulated services must be capped for the first three years at the rates in effect at the time of opt in, except for any plan provisions that address exogenous changes.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) Within 30 days of the electing company filing notice to the commission, interested parties may file comments identifying any aspect of the adoption that the party believes is contrary to the public interest. Reply comments may be filed within 45 days following the notice to the commission. The commission shall accept the adoption unless it finds adoption of the existing plan by the electing telephone company is not in the public interest, in which case it may reject or modify the election to opt into the provisions of the existing plan. If the commission modifies the election, the electing company may withdraw its proposed adoption of the existing plan by filing notice with the commission within 30 days of the commission's modification order.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.766.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.13.0">1995 c 156 s 13</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/223/#laws.0.16.0">1997 c 223 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/214/#laws.0.2.0">2004 c 214 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/254/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 254 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.767">
        <h1 class="shn">237.767 DISCONTINUANCE OF SERVICE.</h1>
        <p>Without the express approval of the commission, a telephone company subject to a plan may not discontinue the provision of a service or basic network function that has been classified as price regulated or flexibly priced.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.767.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.14.0">1995 c 156 s 14</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.768">
        <h1 class="shn">237.768 PERIODIC FINANCIAL REPORT.</h1>
        <p>In addition to the reports required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.766">237.766</a>, an alternative regulation plan may require a telephone company to file with the department an annual report of financial matters for the previous calendar year on or before May 1 of each year on report forms furnished by the department in the same manner as is required of other telephone companies on August 1, 1995. In addition, any company subject to a plan shall file with the commission and department a copy of any filings it has made to the Federal Communications Commission regarding the provisions of video programming provided through a video dial tone facility in Minnesota. An alternative regulation plan may require a telephone company to maintain its accounts in accordance with the system of accounts prescribed for the company by the commission under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.10">237.10</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.768.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.15.0">1995 c 156 s 15</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/4/#laws.6.69.0">1Sp2001 c 4 art 6 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.769">
        <h1 class="shn">237.769 RULES APPLICABLE.</h1>
        <p>Any company under a plan is subject to any rules adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16">237.16</a> on the same date as those rules are applicable to other companies.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.769.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.16.0">1995 c 156 s 16</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/223/#laws.0.17.0">1997 c 223 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.770">
        <h1 class="shn">237.770 SUBSIDIZATION.</h1>
        <p>No telephone company shall subsidize flexibly priced or non-price-regulated services from other services. A telephone service is not subsidized if the aggregate revenues for the service equal or exceed the total service long-run incremental costs of providing the service. If the commission determines, after a proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>, that subsidization exists, it shall order changes in rates to price the subsidized service above total service long-run incremental cost and may invoke any other remedies otherwise available under this chapter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.770.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.17.0">1995 c 156 s 17</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.771">
        <h1 class="shn">237.771 DISCRIMINATION.</h1>
        <p>The rates of a telephone company under a plan must be the same in all geographic locations of the state except for good cause. A plan may contain provisions that define good cause, including consideration of the ability to respond to competition. Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.09">237.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.121">237.121</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.60#stat.237.60.3">237.60, subdivision 3</a>, apply to a telephone company under a plan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.771.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.18.0">1995 c 156 s 18</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.772">
        <h1 class="shn">237.772 COST STUDY METHODOLOGY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.772.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Total service long-run incremental cost.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this chapter, total service long-run incremental cost (TSLRIC) means the total cost to the company of supplying a service, group of services, or basic network function. The term "long-run" means a period of time sufficient so that all inputs are avoidable based on the total increment of service, group of services, or basic network function and includes the relevant costs resulting from the company's decision to provide the service, group of services, or basic network function, holding constant the production levels of all other services, groups of services, or basic network functions provided by the company.</p>
          <p>(b) A telephone company is not required to prepare or file TSLRIC or variable cost studies for all of its services as a prerequisite to filing a plan. However, the commission may order cost studies to be prepared for specific services as a condition of approval of the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.772.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Petition for variable cost study.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent that this section or the commission may require a company to provide a TSLRIC study, a company may submit a petition to the commission for permission to submit a variable cost study instead of a TSLRIC study. The commission shall grant the petition if the telephone company demonstrates:</p>
          <p>(1) that a TSLRIC study is burdensome in relation to its annual revenue from the service involved;</p>
          <p>(2) in the case of an existing service, that the service is no longer being offered to new customers; or</p>
          <p>(3) if the telephone company shows other good cause.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.772.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.19.0">1995 c 156 s 19</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.773">
        <h1 class="shn">237.773 ALTERNATIVE REGULATION FOR SMALL TELEPHONE COMPANY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.773.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "small telephone company" means a local exchange telephone company with fewer than 50,000 subscribers that has made an election under subdivision 2 whether or not the company is subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.59">237.59</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.60">237.60, subdivisions 1, 2, and 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.773.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Election; effect.</span></h2>
          <p>A local telephone company with fewer than 50,000 subscribers may elect to become a small telephone company by notice to the commission, in writing, of its decision. The small telephone company may not revoke its election for three years after making the election. While that election remains in effect, a small telephone company is not subject to the rate-of-return regulation or earnings investigation provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.075">237.075</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>.</p>
          <p>If, before electing under this subdivision, a small telephone company has been found by the commission to have significant quality of service problems in violation of applicable commission rules, that company must either resolve the quality of service problems or develop a plan to resolve the quality of service problems in conformance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.765">237.765</a>. The quality of service plan must be approved by the commission in order for an election under this subdivision to be effective. The commission shall make a determination on the quality of service plan within 60 days after it is submitted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.773.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local rate.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), a small telephone company shall not implement a rate increase for any service listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.761#stat.237.761.3">237.761, subdivision 3</a>, beyond the level in effect 60 days prior to an election under subdivision 2, until the later of January 1, 1998, or two years after making an election. However, a small telephone company may implement any new service and establish rates for any new service and may change rates for any other service at any time subject to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.761#stat.237.761.4">237.761, subdivision 4</a>. A small company shall provide to its customers the ability to block, at no extra charge, any new service which it offers, provides, or bills. This requirement shall not apply to services that require affirmative subscription by the customer. Nothing in this section shall prevent the commission from requiring blocking or other privacy or safety protections for other types of telecommunications services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) At any time following one year after electing under subdivision 2, a small telephone company may change rates for local services except switched network access services, listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.761#stat.237.761.3">237.761, subdivision 3</a>, to reflect:</p>
          <p>(1) changes in state and federal taxes;</p>
          <p>(2) changes in jurisdictional allocations from the Federal Communications Commission, the amount of which the small telephone company cannot control and for which equal and opposite exogenous changes are made on the federal level;</p>
          <p>(3) substantial financial impacts of investments in network upgrades which are made; or</p>
          <p>(i) if the investment exceeds 20 percent of the gross plant investment of the company; or</p>
          <p>(ii) as the result of government mandates to construct specific telephone infrastructure, if the mandate applies to local telephone companies and the company would not otherwise be compensated.</p>
          <p class="in">A small telephone company may change rates for local services listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.761#stat.237.761.3">237.761, subdivision 3</a>, at any time, to implement extended area service or any successor to that service on an income-neutral basis.</p>
          <p class="in">A small telephone company proposing an increase under this subdivision shall provide 60 days' advance written notice to the department and each of the company's customers including the individual rates affected and the procedure necessary for the customers to petition for investigation. If the department receives a petition within 45 days after the notice from five percent or 500, whichever is fewer, of the customers of the small telephone company, the department and the company shall jointly determine if the petition is valid and, if so, may investigate the rate change to determine if it conforms to the limitations of this subdivision. Within 30 days of validating the petition, the department shall report its findings to the commission, which shall either adopt the report or order changes to conform to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(c) On or after the later of January 1998, or two years after making an election under subdivision 2, a small telephone company may increase rates for local services, except switched network access services, listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.761#stat.237.761.3">237.761, subdivision 3</a>. A small telephone company proposing an increase shall provide 60 days' advance written notice to its customers including individual rates affected and the procedure necessary for the customers to petition for investigation. If the commission receives a petition within 45 days after such notice, from five percent or 500, whichever is fewer, of the customers of the small telephone company, the department and the company shall jointly determine if the petition is valid and, if so, may investigate the proposed rate increase to determine if it is appropriate in light of rates charged by other local exchange telephone companies for comparable services, taking into account calling scope, quality of service, the availability of competitive alternatives, service costs, and the features available to the customers. Within 30 days of validating the petition, the department shall file a report with the commission which shall then approve appropriate rates for those services. Rates established by the commission under this paragraph shall not be increased within one year of implementation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.773.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Access rate.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No election by a small telephone company may in any way change the terms or conditions of any interexchange access charge settlements approved by the commission before an election under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) While any interexchange access charge settlement approved by the commission remains in effect, the commission and department shall enforce the agreement without further investigation of interexchange access charges or earnings relating to the interexchange access service. Except as specifically provided in this section, the commission retains all of its authority under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.081">237.081</a> to investigate other matters relating to interexchange access charges and to issue appropriate orders, and the department retains its authority under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216A.07">216A.07</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.15">237.15</a> to investigate matters relating to interexchange access charges.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.773.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/436/#laws.0.3.0">2000 c 436 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.773.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.20.0">1995 c 156 s 20</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.50.0">2001 c 7 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/214/#laws.0.3.0">2004 c 214 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.774">
        <h1 class="shn">237.774 APPLICATION OF OTHER LAWS.</h1>
        <p>Except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.773">237.773</a>, a telephone company subject to a plan approved under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.764">237.764</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.773">237.773</a>, shall comply with any state or federal laws governing the provision of telephone services. Nothing contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.76">237.76</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.773">237.773</a> is intended in any way to change or modify the definitions contained in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.01">237.01</a> or what constitutes the provision of telephone service under this chapter or other laws.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.774.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/156/#laws.0.21.0">1995 c 156 s 21</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/97/#laws.0.2.0">2003 c 97 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.775">
        <h1 class="shn">237.775 EXISTING PLAN NOT AFFECTED.</h1>
        <p>An alternative regulation plan approved by the commission prior to May 1, 1997, is not subject to the amendments in Laws 1997, chapter 223; provided that a plan filed, revised, or renewed after that date is subject to those amendments.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.775.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/223/#laws.0.18.0">1997 c 223 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TELEPHONE COMPANY WITH CABLE SERVICES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.79">
        <h1 class="shn">237.79 TELEPHONE COMPANY PROVIDING CABLE SERVICE.</h1>
        <p>A telephone company that provides cable television services shall, with respect to provisioning of those services in Minnesota, be subject to the same franchise requirements, procedures, and fees, and public, educational, and government access requirements as a cable communication company under chapter 238.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/123/#laws.0.6.0">1997 c 123 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">INTEREXCHANGE TELEPHONE SERVICE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.80">
        <h1 class="shn">237.80 INTEREXCHANGE TELEPHONE SERVICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.80.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.1.58.0">2014 c 222 art 1 s 58</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.80.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consultation with FCC.</span></h2>
          <p>Any investigation or proceeding by the Minnesota Public Utilities Commission for the purpose of verifying compliance with the competitive checklist requirements of section 271(c) of the act must be completed by the commission and the resulting certification provided to the Federal Communications Commission within 90 days after receipt of a request for verification from the Federal Communications Commission.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.80.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/445/#laws.0.1.0">1996 c 445 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.81">
        <h1 class="shn">237.81 SCOPE.</h1>
        <p>To the extent they regulate telecommunications right-of-way users, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.04">237.04</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.16#stat.237.16.1">237.16, subdivision 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.162">237.162</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.163">237.163</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.74#stat.237.74.5">237.74, subdivision 5</a>, supersede section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/222.37">222.37</a>, and any ordinance, regulation, or rule to the contrary.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/123/#laws.0.7.0">1997 c 123 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/69/#laws.3.19.0">2005 c 69 art 3 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.82">
        <h1 class="shn">237.82 DEFINITIONS; CALL LOCATION INFORMATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.82.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/237.83">237.83</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.82.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Call location information.</span></h2>
          <p>"Call location information" means information indicating the geographical location of a telecommunications device.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.82.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Law enforcement agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Law enforcement agency" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a unit of state or local government that is authorized by law to grant full powers of arrest and to charge a person with the duties of preventing and detecting crime and enforcing the general criminal laws of the state; and</p>
          <p>(2) subject to the limitations in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.93">626.93</a>, a law enforcement agency of a federally recognized tribe, as defined in United States Code, title 25, section 450b(e).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.82.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Wireless telecommunications service provider.</span></h2>
          <p>"Wireless telecommunications service provider" means a provider of commercial mobile radio services, as that term is defined in United States Code, title 47, section 332, subsection (d), including all broadband personal communications services, wireless radio telephone services, geographic area specialized and enhanced specialized mobile radio services, and incumbent wide area specialized mobile radio licensees, that offers real-time, two-way voice service interconnected with the public switched telephone network and that is doing business in this state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/342/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 342 s 1</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.237.83">
        <h1 class="shn">237.83 DISCLOSURE OF CALL LOCATION INFORMATION; EMERGENCY SITUATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.83.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Written request.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of a written request from a law enforcement agency stating that the disclosure of call location information is needed in an emergency situation that involves the risk of death or serious physical harm to a person who possesses a telecommunications device, a wireless telecommunications service provider shall provide the requested call location information concerning that device to the requesting agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.83.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Protocols.</span></h2>
          <p>A wireless telecommunications service provider shall establish protocols consistent with this section that govern its response to a request from a law enforcement agency under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.83.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cause of action limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>No cause of action shall lie in any court against a wireless telecommunications service provider, its officers, employees, agents, or other specified persons for providing call location information while acting in good faith and according to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.237.83.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/212/#laws.4.3.0">2014 c 212 art 4 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.237.83.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/342/#laws.0.2.0">2010 c 342 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
